## Goose

The goose  was a domesticated species of fowl, widely bred across the Realms and had many uses—from food to pillow stuffing.

Description
A well-fattened goose before slaughter could fetch a price between 8 silver coins and 1 gold.

Species of Geese
Grey Vast geese were a species of wild goose hunted and served in the city of Ravens Bluff.
Wild game geese were known to inhabit the forests of Cormanthor and the Sea of Fallen Stars.

Ecology

Geese were domesticated and used as a food source not only by humanoids, but also verbeegs.

Habitats
Geese were widely domesticated across the Realms, including Shadowdale, Phlan in the Moonsea region, Selgaunt in Sembia, Turmish, Moonshae Isles, Delimbiyr Vale, Hartsvale of the Ice Spires, Innarlith in the Lake of Steam region, the Silver Marches, Rashemen, Thar, Halruaa, and the ancient city of Cursrah in the Calim Empire, as well as Calimshan.

Usages

Items
Arrows were fletched with goose feathers in Calimshan and Turmish.
Goose down was used as mattress stuffing, notably in the guest rooms of the Old Skull Inn of Shadowdale, and pillow stuffing.
Quills were crafted out of geese feathers, dating as far back as the days of ancient Netheril.
Grease of goose fat, when mixed with sand and soot, could be used to age and discolor wooden items, such as wands.
Goose grease was used as boots polish by the Rashemaar.

Food
Goose eggs were cooked over-easy with bacon for morningfeast in the Moonshae Isles.
Goose eggs with fried bread was a typical morningfeast in Shadowdale and served at the Old Skull Inn.
Various eggs, including geese eggs, were cooked to guests' taste and served with sausage and breading nests in the Dripping Dagger of Waterdeep. This dish was valued at 5 cp.
The dish called egg platter from the Mosstonian festhall Dryad's Dreams consisted of various fowl eggs served in spices and dips.
In Delimbiyr Vale, goose roast was served with stewed tomatos, pickled beets, and dried apples.
In Selgaunt, Sembian nut-roasted goose was a luxurious dish served at the nobles' events.
The Riverman inn at the Blackfeather Bridge in Sembia served bean-and-goose pies.
In Shadowdale, geese were roasted in gravy and served with ale.
The town of Glister in Thar served roasted goose accompanied by scalloped potatoes, warm fresh bread, and wine.
Gurneth's Goblet of Ravens Bluff served pricy smoked goose in curried sausage, leek, shrimp, and ginger sauce.
Goose pies were a common type of pastry in Cormyr.
The Sleeping Cat restaurant in Wheloon, Cormyr, served wine-simmered goose.
Goose liver paté was served smeared on crusty bread in the city of Sundabar, Silver Marches.
Essembrian recipe of the stuffed turkey pie called for a handful of chopped goose giblets.
Steamed goose was served in taverns of Hillsfar.
 Cured goose and pickleberries were enjoyed by Marlamin and Gwinn of Longsaddle.

Magic
Virgin goose down feathers could be used as a material component of a personal shielding spell.
Simbul knew a way of turning a brightly-colored goose egg into a deadly enchanted grenade-like thrown weapon. When the shell cracked, a magical bubble expanded around it, pushing out all air, quick suffocating any breathing creatures trapped inside.
Mazticas pluma magic used goose dawn as material component. Such spells included warmth and water move.

History
By the mid‒14 century DR, several dozen geese inhabited a pond in front of Caer Windlauer, a castle in a cometary orbit around the Sun in Realmspace.

By 1358 DR, famed hin Harper agent Olive Ruskettle came to realization that that her favorite dish was roasted goose (not horse feed) and drink—Luiren Rivengut (not water in a pail), after spending some time polymorphed into a burro.

In 1368 DR, an ex-Harper Gorion was killed with a goose feather-fletched arrow to his eye by Sarevok Anchev and his goods, sending Abdel Adrian on a fateful journey of discovering the truth of his divine birth.

In the late 14 century DR, Lanaal Featherbreeze a sun elf from Evereska was known to transform herself into a giant goose to aid her companions.

Cultural Relevance
 In 1360 DR, Hsuan Yu Wu knew a martial technique called the angry goose nerve kick, that rendered the opponent's body too sore to move. The waved of agony caused by the technique lasted for an hour. She used the kick against Batu Min Ho, her husband, after mistaking him for an intruder.
 Being said to be a goose or "goose-witted" was a derogatory term used by inhabitants of Waterdeep and the Moonsea that meant "dim-witted".
 Golden Goose Casion was located onboard of the Grand Dame vessel in Yartar circa 1485 DR.

## Gorgon mud

Gorgon mud, referred to in Jotun as fjellblod by the stone giant thane Kayalithica, was a variety of magical mud that carried a petrification effect.

Description
This substance was described by some as being viscous and green, forming pockets within areas of soft ground.

Properties
This substance behaved similar to quicksand, gradually dragging victims down into it. When a sinking creature became immersed in gorgon mud, it would become subject to petrification.

Gorgon mud radiated a detectable aura of transmutation magic. When removed from its pool, the substance's magical properties became inert.

Availability
 Gorgon mud could be found within various alcoves of Deadstone Cleft, where it long acted as a defensive measure for the stone giants that lived there.
 Beyond the Prime Material plane, it was not uncommon to encounter gorgon mud in the Elemental Chaos and in Molor, the 528 layer of the Abyss ruled by Juiblex.

## Gorynych

Gorynychs were ferocious, draconic monsters that would attempt to eat almost anything they came across.

Description
Gorynychs were massive creatures, some  long snout-to-tail, and weighed . They had appropriately massive wings, and, most distinctively, three wolfish heads and three tails that each split into many whiplike ends (as many as twelve ends had been recorded).

Behavior
Gorynychs were ferocious and bestial creatures, but cunning.

Abilities
Gorynychs lacked the magical abilities, the fear aura, and the breath weapon of true dragons, but their sheer physical might tended to make up for the lack.

Combat
Gorynychs were terrors in combat, using their three heads, claws, and the six largest tail-whips with a bestial cunning and ferocity that meant they had been recorded shredding enemies with their claws, grappling them with their tails, and even ripping victims in half between two heads. Additionally, cutting off a tail or head did little to slow the beasts down.

Ecology

Gorynychs were unusual in their biology in that, while each head had a brain capable of handling all bodily functions on its own, the three brains had only one mind shared between them. Thus, the gorynych had only one mind (and the heads could not be turned against each other), but all three heads slept at once. They also could not be surprised from behind thanks to both their sharp hearing and flexible heads.

Gorynychs preferred to live in windy, desolate mountains, making their lairs in great caverns, which were often littered with claw marks, shed scales, the occasional treasure, and bones. They avoided regions where humans were frequent but would make note of any nearby roadways or isolated settlements.

Gorynychs laid eggs, and hatchlings were driven out of the nest almost immediately after hatching. They attained full growth in only nine years, and gorynychs could live as long as four centuries.

They were carnivores and typically the apex predator of their territory. They had no language of their own and minimal understanding of whatever the dominant language in the area was, and they rarely interacted with other intelligent species. Other magically powerful and competitive creatures such as other dragons and beholders were known to attack them.

Gorynychs had been recorded living in northeastern Faerun.

## Golden eggs of Ghastar

The golden eggs of Ghastar were a collection of powerful magical items created in Tabot in ancient times.

Description
They were nine golden eggs, each of them unique and not all of them gold. They took the form of eggs, of course, adorned with animals or inlays and fashioned from precious metals, stones, and gems.

Powers
Each egg had its own distinct magical power, usable only by a monk or shukenja of lawful nature and moderate to high ability. The powers of the extant eggs were as follows:
 The gold egg with ruby snakes could stop avalanches and floods,
 The obsidian egg with silver crocodiles could create earthquakes (like the earthquake spell),
 The gold egg supported by jade elephants could control people's minds (as with mass charm), and
 The silver egg with emerald inlays resembling lace could trigger a volcanic eruption.
The five lost eggs were thought to be equally powerful, but the information was lost to history.

History
The nine golden eggs were crafted by artisans of ancient Tabot, long before the coming of the lamas in . Even their original name seems to have been lost—Ghastar was only the last man known to have possessed all nine.

Over the centuries, however, the eggs became scattered and five of them were lost. By, the four remaining eggs were held by the lamas at Pokarr Gompa. When Rokstang was inundated by a great flood in, the Abbot of Pokarr, Mo Kin, used the ruby-snake egg to stop the flood and save the city.

Notable Owners
 Ghastar, the last to own all nine golden eggs and for whom they were later named.
 The lamas of Pokarr Gompa, who kept the remaining four in its treasure rooms around .

References

## Golemmaster

A golemmaster was an elite specialty priest of the Church of Azuth whose work focused primarily on golems.

Activities
As their name implied, a golemmaster's life's work was the construction and maintenance of golems. They were also sworn to destroy any rogue golems.

Culture
A golemmaster viewed the construction of each golem as a prayer to Azuth. As such, they strove for utter perfection with each of their creations.

Abilities
Golemmasters were quite artistic. They were able to construct a golem in half the time, and for half of the price, of an ordinary individual once they attained a certain degree of expertise. If a golemmaster had access to the appropriate Manual of golems, then this was further decreased to a quarter of the time and cost.

Given their intimate knowledge of golem construction, these individuals found it much easier to strike and harm golems, regardless of the weapon they used. This included being able to harm golems with nonmagical weapons. A rod of smiting wielded by a golemmaster stood a higher chance of completely destroying a golem when struck.

Berserk golems always selected a golemmaster as their primary target.

Possessions
These individuals usually wore the common clothes and aprons of artisans and were usually covered in the evidence of their trade, be it stone dust, iron filings, etc.

A golemmaster would wear either leather or studded leather armor during combat. For weaponry they preferred warhammers or staves.

Relationships
Many believed that golemmasters were touched by a hint of the madness attributed to Azuth.

Appendix
References

## Golden Gull

The Golden Gull was a merchant ship owned by the Irlentree patriar family that sank at the Tower of Storms in the late 15 century DR.

Description
The ship was about  long with a single mast that stood over  high, which was all that was visible of the ship above water after it was wrecked. The remains of the crew were scattered about the ship, their bodies having been picked clean by fish. The murky water was home sharks such as the fearsome Daggermaw.

History
As of 1492 DR, Hitoshi Jade was a sailor who worked aboard the Golden Gull and frequented the Low Lantern.

The Golden Gull was sunk when it was lured onto the rocks beneath the Tower of Storms by the bewitching magic of Moesko—an anchorite of Talos—in service to efforts by the Cult of Talos to plunder ships along the Sword Coast North in the late 15 century DR. However, a chest containing a trove of electrum pieces and valuable pearls were never recovered from the wreck.

## Golden dragonmail

Golden dragonmail was a spell used by sorcerers and wizards.

Effects
When cast, this spell created a suit of  of radiant golden plate mail on the caster. Sketched on the plates were patterns of dragonscale, mimicking a gold dragon. The caster could also choose to have the symbol of Torm sketched on the plate. If the caster removed the suit of armor, or it was destroyed, the spell expired. If the caster was already wearing armor, the spell had no effect.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, a coin of solid gold and mithral in the shape of a dragonscale.

## Gori

Gori was a Neverwintan soldier stationed in the Leilon garrison in the High Tower of Thalivar in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Gori was a timid dwarven soldier.

History
When Leilon was resettled in the late 15 century DR, Dagult Neverember sent some of Neverwinter's soldiers to defend the settlers during the town's reconstruction. Gori was among these soldiers, serving under Sergeant Hazz Yorrum's command. The High Tower of Thalivar acted as a garrison for the soldiers. However, it turned out that the wizard tower was haunted by the ghost of Thalivar. The wizard's spirit drove away Gori, Yorrum and two other soldiers (Erlum and Koz). However, the town mage Gallio Elibro refused to leave. The soldiers had recently noticed Gallio's odd behavior but they were unaware that Thalivar's ghost took possession of the mage every night. A group of adventurers took care of the ghost.

Besides being edible, goosefoot could be used to make a sedative. Particularly one that treated hysteria or nervous tension.

## Goblin's Grog

The so-called Goblin's Grog was a magical intoxicating drink brewed by goblins of the Delimbiyr Vale.

Description
This grog drink possessed an attractive color and not-so-attractive odor. Some described the brew's smell as a potent mix of odoriferous two-week-old cabbage and the foul stench of a bear's armpit.

Powers
Apart from being intoxicating, the brew functioned as a potion of invisibility.

History
In the, goblins of the Delimbiyr Vale were enrolled into servitude to the orcs and tanarukka army of the Devil of Daggerford–Gorgomorz. The goblins brewed their foul grog and generously imbibed it. When drunk and invisible, they ventured into the Wild Pastures where they fed the magical grog to cattle. To observers, it appeared as if the cows simply vanished into thin air in the middle of the day.

## Golden Fox Martial Arts School

The Golden Fox Martial Arts School was a once-renowned school in the city of Akkaido on the island of Tsukishima in Wa.

History
While the school was open, all individuals who reached the rank of the Master were gifted special enchanted Golden Bamboo Staves. The school's end came in the early-to-mid-14 century DR, then the Masters of the Golden Fox came into a conflict with the notorious Yakuza. By the, the Yakuza emerged victorious in the conflict, and to ensure the Golden Fox Martial Arts School would never be reestablished, they hunted down and destroyed the Golden Bamboo Staves. Even though there were seven in existence, at least one of the weapons was saved and taken out of Wa.

## Golden Bamboo Staff

Golden Bamboo Staves were enchanted staves used by Masters of the the Golden Fox Martial Arts School in the Waan city of Akkaido.

Powers
These weapons possessed a +2 enchantment and almost certainly caused their targets to become stunned for 18 seconds upon a successful strike.

History
Golden Bamboo Staves came from the island of Tsukishima in the eastern land of Kara-Tur. Each member of the Golden Fox Martial Arts School received one of these staves as they reached the status of a Master. Several years before the,  the Golden Fox clashed with the infamous criminal organization - the Yakuza. Subsequently, the Yakuza prevailed, and the martial arts academy was disbanded. To ensure their enemies did not reestablish the Golden Fox School, members of the Yakuza tracked down and destroyed seven Golden Bamboo Staves. However, it was unknown how many weapons survived the conflict, but one of them left Wa and was exported to the Dragon Coast in 1374 DR. The staff was in the inventory of Okuzo's Imports, a shop located in the Harbor Loop of Westgate.

## Goggles of minute seeing

Goggles of minute seeing were enchanted Lantanese helmets, often used by the island's archeologists.

Description
Goggles in name only, these magical helmets, or more precisely, metallurgist's faceguard helms, were designed in a distinctly Lantanese fashion. The helmet's front had an intricate series of lenses that could be rotated and placed in front of the wearer's eyes.

Powers
The helmet's lenses allowed the wearer to see even the smallest details up-close. These magic lenses made the goggles of minute seeing invaluable when examining one's environment or trying to identify strange and possibly dangerous items.

History
This particular type of helmet was created in Lantan to be used by archeologists when dealing with various contraptions of old. By the late 14 century DR, these helmets gained some popularity along the Sword Coast and even the Nelanther Isles.

As of the late 15 century DR, versions of the goggles existed with applications in combat.

Notable Owners
 Naomlen Girdaur, a dwarven rogue active in Waterdeep in the late 15 century DR.

## Goldspires

The abbey of Goldspires was the main center of Waukeen worship in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Location
Goldspires was located north-west of the town of Athkatla, outside the city walls. It overlooked the Merchant's Bay from a seacoast promontory, above the city skyline.

Structure

Goldspires was a massive fortified castle-abbey with high gold-plated gates. With over 15 buildings within its walls, it was almost a small city in its own right. Its towers were decorated with gilded conical roofs,

Among the recorded buildings of Goldspires were:
 A dance hall
 The Hospitality House, rented by rich patrons to host galas
 An infirmary
 Stables
 Several warehouses
 Several office buildings

Services
Most temple services were revels, parties and lavish banquets, funded by the faithful's contributions.

History
Following the Time of Troubles, priests of Goldspires were among the first ones to accept the message of the prophet of Lliira and restore an active faith in their deity.

Rumors & Legends
Goldspires was said to be supported by twelve major mercantile companies and powerful Amnian families, including seven rising merchant houses, who hoped to gain power thanks to the temple's influence.

Inhabitants
The temple was ruled by the Holycoin Voice of the Lady Tharundar Olehm, the supreme head of the church. The high priest was seconded by five Overgold sisters referred to as the Five Furies, all potential successors to his title.

## Gondsman (specialty priest)

A Gondsman was a specialty priest of the Church of Gond in the 14 century DR.

Abilities
The major spheres that Gondsmen had access to for spells were all, astral, combat, divination, elemental, numbers, protection, sun, and wards. The minor spheres that they had access to for spells were charm, creation, guardian, healing, and necromantic. Some particular spells they knew how to cast included animate object, probing arm, stoneskin, thunder staff, tree steed, and unseen servant.

Unlike the specialty priests of some faiths, Gondsmen could neither command nor turn undead.

Beyond their magical abilities, Gondsmen were often proficient armorers, blacksmiths, carpenters, engineers, gemcutters, leatherworkers, miners, potters, stonemasons, or weaponsmiths. They also all had an aptitude for all manner of devices, including mechanical locks.

Possessions
Gondsmen often included lockpicks on the crimson sashes they wore, which were adorned in various tools and equipment. The armor they wore ranged as good as plate mail, though they never wielded shields. In terms of weaponry they favored bludgeoning weapons, crossbows, and firearms if they were available, such as the arquebus.

History
Following the Time of Troubles, Gondsmen were chiefly the ones responsible for the manufacture and proliferation of firearms across Faerûn, shipping them to western ports. Over time they created and introduced the arquebus, blunderbuss, and ribald.

Circa 1370 DR,Faiths & Avatars makes reference to a past event occurring in  on page 46. the proportion of Gondsmen on the island nation of Lantan was about twenty for every cleric, while throughout the rest of Faerûn the proportion of clerics to Gondsmen was typically 15 to 1. The majority of Gondsmen found in Faerûn were merchants from Lantan. Around this time gnomes were also beginning to take up this vocation, though it was frowned upon in most gnomish communities.

Following the Spellplague, "Gondsmen" became the preferred term for both worshipers and members of the Church of Gond's clergy.

## Golden Tankard

The Golden Tankard was the local tavern found in the village of Parnast as of the late 15 century DR. It was run by Raggnar Redtooth, an amiable but unscrupulous asset of the Cult of the Dragon.

Description
The tavern earned its name from a particular golden tankard of plenty of dwarven origin found by its proprietor. Images of reveling dwarves and patterns of shocks of grain decorated the magical mug.

Location
It was situated on the south side of the village, just on the edge of the main square and central well.

Services
The Tankard offered mugs of ale and mead, for 3 cp or 1 sp respectively, and hearty food like stewed cabbage, black bread, and sausages. There were no beds available for rent at the tavern.

Locals were served with no extra concern, but travelers were merely offered service and reports about their activities were given to a high-ranking Dragon Cult agent, Captain Othelstan.

History
At some point in the 1480s DR, the Cult of the Dragon used the Golden Tankard as rallying point as they amassed a trove of wealth and riches stolen from various locales across the Sword Coast.

Inhabitants
 Gundalin, Parnast's local wheelwright was a regular at the Golden Tankard.

## Gorey Eye-Beast

The Gorey Eye-Beast was a rough tavern located in the city of Zhentil Keep during the mid-14 century DR before that city's destruction.

Location
The tavern was located within Zhentil Keep's Common Quarter in the southeast section of the city at the intersection of Beholder Street and Moonsea Avenue.

Description
The sign hanging outside of the tavern depicted an eye tyrant whose fangs were dripping blood.

Inhabitants
The mispelling of "gory" in the tavern's name served as an accurate preview of the crude clientele found within. The Gorey Eye-Beast catered mostly to wicked humanoids such as orcs, ogres, and hobgoblins. Any humans or demihumans entering the tavern were met by a half dozen ogres armed with clubs. Anyone skilled enough to make it past the ogre bouncers were allowed to stay, though they were watched carefully.

## Gorothir's Girdle

Gorothir's Girdle was a holy text of the church of Shar that took the unusual form of a black leather corset instead of an actual book. It was said to be one of the oldest garments in Faerûn.

Description
The Girdle was a black leather corset studded with many small figures carved out of jet. There were a total of 28 figures decorating it, each of them as tall as a small man's palm and sporting empty eye sockets. Those visages were particularly life-like, and were easily recognizable from each other. They were described as follows:
A laughing lady • a sad old bearded man • a jester • a knight • a fat woman • a hook-nosed man • a cowled man • a bearded man in a feather cap • a girl child • a weeping lady • a grim scarred warrior • a scowling dwarf • a sneering elf • a crying boy • a baby • an idiot man • a whistling man • a man with his tongue sticking out • a staring man • a bald man • a howling man • a man with many tufts of hair • an alluring lady • a woman with her tongue sticking out • a screaming woman • a smiling lady • a jolly man • a woman with scaly skin

While made to be worn by an individual with a small waist, the laces holding the Girdle together allowed almost anyone to wear it—albeit sometimes in a ridiculous fashion.

Powers
This sacred item was protected by enchantments against deliberate attacks and the ravages of time, making it as resistant as bone or ivory. It also benefited from a permanent ironguard spell.

The knowledge contained in the Girdle could be revealed by placing the correct gems in the jet figures' empty eye sockets. When a correct gem was inserted, it would stick in place and glow slightly. When a wrong gem was used it would vanish, consumed by the divine item. Once both gems in a specific figure were matched properly the corresponding spell would appear within that face in midair, in vertical floating letters of green fire. Doing so would not consume the gems, which were safeguarded by the divine relic's protective powers and could only be removed deliberately.

While typical holy books could only be used by faithful of the corresponding deity, Shar allowed the Girdle to be used by priests of any faith—even against her own clergy. On occasions, Shar proved capable of corrupting the possessor of the girdle if they were not her followers already, turning them into faithful servants. She would also take actions if she deemed the current owner of the item to be too weak, whispering to more worthy priests so they could make the girdle their own.

In some cases, the Girdle granted its owner the ability to cast spells once a day such as aberrate, damning darkness, fiendish clarity, and slow consumption. It would also sometimes damages creatures of good alignment that made contact with it.

Contents

As a spellbook, the Girdle contained information on 28 spells, which were represented by the many visages ornamenting it:
animate dead, armor of darkness, blade barrier, circle of privacy, crawling darkness, creature of darkness, crushing walls, dark road, darkbolt, dispel magic, dissension's feast, efficacious monster ward, greater creature of darkness, grounding, heal, hovering road, imbue with spell ability, invisibility to undead, meld into stone, mind read, mindshatter, miscast magic, mistaken missive, modify memory, monster mount, obscurement, telepathy, and whip of Shar.

On the inside of the girdle, a tale described how Gorothir felled an empire with only a lie and a secret. This text also contained the specifications of the false sending spell. Some sages believe that this text wasn't originally present on the girdle, but was an addition by a former owner.

History
The Girdle was first used circa  by Gorothir, the Dark Prophet of the Night, a priest of Shar. While some believed that Gorothir made the item, others thought he received it from the goddess or from one of her servants.

This belt had a bloody history, moving from one owner to the next through murder and deceit. Gorothir lost the belt after being blasted in a conflagration of powerful magic by his descendants. One of those took the belt for himself, only to be assassinated by a fellow clergywoman a couple years later. One particular nameless owner, who displeased Shar, was turned into a staggering undead to watch over the Girdle until a more worthy owner showed up. The belt was lost in the Underdark after a powerful priestess attained lichdom but lost Shar's favor. Its last known owner was Zesstra Aleanrahel, third daughter of a powerful drow matron mother.

Rumors
 The Girdle was said to be haunted by the spirits of some of its famous owners, such as Rulgond and Klaunauthe.
 It was said amongst the clergy of Shar that secret combinations of gem placements could let the owner summon some of the spirits haunting the belt, of even the presence of the goddess herself, to answer questions and provide advice.
 It was rumored that a 29 spell could be learned from the girdle, by placing the correct sort of stone in all eye sockets. It was unclear which stone should be used, but probably some lesser ornamental variety. The 29 spell was supposed to be mass resurrection.
 The belt was said to have appeared in markets and shops regularly and sold at a very high price, only to disappear again not long afterwards.

Notable Owners
The known owners of the Girdle are listed below in chronological order.
 Gorothir, the first owner, rumored to be the creator of the belt (circa )
 Jurguth Goroth, a descendant of Gorothir (circa 446 DR)
 Klaunauthe Draeyl, a priestess of Shar (circa 469 DR)
 The adventurer Rulgond (circa 554 DR, until its death in the  from the blades of fellow adventurers)
 Lalondra Worul, a priestess of Shar sent by the goddess to retrieve the belt after it went through several nameless hands (circa 617 DR). She lost the belt after achieving lichdom in the  and being deserted by Shar.
 Zesstra Aleanrahel, a female drow who recovered the Girdle from the body of a dead illithilich and converted to the worship of Shar (circa 1370 DR).

Appendix
References

## Gorothir

Gorothir the Dark Prophet of the Night was a powerful high priest of Shar during the time of the Netherese empire.

Description
Gorothir was said to be darkly handsome, and sired countless children over his centuries-long life.

Personality
Gorothir was a cunning and energetically evil man, feared even by his fellow faithful. He liked to indulge in sensual excesses and orgies of violence, and was given seemingly endless power by his goddess. Gorothir ruled his church and the lands it controlled like a mad tyrant.

Abilities
The Dark Prophet was said to be able to turn into a man-stag twice as tall as a man. He was also said to be able to twist spells cast against him, causing their magical powers to go haywire.

Possessions
Gorothir possessed two objects which were considered as sacred relic by the followers of the Dark Lady:
Gorothir's Girdle
 A magical corset of great power and one of the church of Shar's holy books.

The Shadowstaff of Gorothir
 A magical staff capable of manipulating shadow magic, summoning various creatures and casting shadow bolts.

Relationships
Gorothir's gave his name to his descendants, which were known as the Goroth family. One of those descendants, Jurguth Goroth, was involved in his ancestor's death and snatched up Gorothir's Girdle afterwards.

History
Gorothir was a powerful priest of Shar, active around the Fall of Netheril. Around the, he came into possession of a divine relic of the Mistress of the Night—the eponymous girdle. The origin of the Girdle was unclear: some believed that the powerful priest created it, whereas others believed it was gifted to her high priest by the Dark Lady herself.

Due to his tremendous power and tyrannical nature, Gorothir was feared as much as he was hated. During the  his grandchilden allied with several of his enemies, from disgruntled mages to rulers whose thrones were usurped. The motley alliance ambushed him while he was raiding a place of power sacred to Selûne, during an event which would later be known as the Night of Dark Fire. They managed to kill him in a massive conflagration of magic which killed most of them as well. After this victory an archmage of Selûne shattered the staff of the high priest, losing his life in the process. As for his belt, it was salvaged by one of his descendants and played a major part in the history of the Church of Shar afterwards.

During the, the whispers of the Dark Goddess led Lord Vanrak Moonstar to find the remains of Gorothir and of his staff. The Dark Ranger built a crypt to store those blessed relics in his base of Vanrakdoom, deep in Undermountain. The relics were kept in a sarcophagus resting on the desecrated Plinth Stone of the Moonmaiden. To reward this act of great devotion, Shar restored the Shadowstaff to its former power and sent a couple angels of decay to watch over the relics.

Rumors & Legends
It was said that Gorothir felled an empire with only a lie and a secret.

Appendix
References

---

## Golden Tree of Dawn

## Goblin arrow

Goblin arrows, despite their name, were not missiles used by goblins, but a type of enchanted arrow created by a black-hearted gnome Turbar Talinbar.

Powers
Even though these arrows possessed +1 enchantment, they were now known for their damaging power. The magics imbued into goblin arrows were activated once a missile hit a target. It summoned forth an incredibly agitated and hostile goblin. The monstrous humanoid remained there for the next seventeen minutes before being sent back from wherever the creature was pulled from.

History
Goblin arrows were invented sometime before the, by an evil gnome Turbar Talinbar. Since then, these items have become known for being an invaluable method of distraction or annoyance. Most commonly, goblin arrows could be found in the hands of mischievous halflings and elves. However, several adventuring troupes have used a number of these arrows before entering challenging battles.

## Goblin totem

Goblin totems were magical weapons crafted by goblins out of the bones of powerful monsters.

Description
These weapons were generally crude. They were either fashioned or carved from bone, and had leather strips wrapped around the intended handle. At least among goblins, these objects were as storied as any legendary weapons about which humans told tales amongst themselves.

Powers
Wielding a goblin totem filled one with confidence that they could face enemies much larger than themselves, and the weapons were indeed were more deadly against larger opponents. Big hits also tended to hit a bit harder with a goblin totem.

Notable Goblin Totems
 The horn totem and skull totem were fashioned in the 15 century DR from the bones of an oni known as the Ogre King by his goblin worshipers.
 A goblin totem was in the possession of the Cloudrippers tribe in the Tannath Mountains of Aglarond.

The Golden Bowl was a big temple, being home to more than forty priests.

The uppermost floor of the temple was unroofed, and magically heated. Plants and flowers cultivated there would bloom even in the middle of winter, and were distributed to the townspeople to lift their spirit.

## Golden Forge

The Golden Forge was a secret temple dedicated to Geb, the Father Under the Skies and Sands within the Mulhorandi pantheon.

Location
The Golden Forge was located within a volcano on the island known as the Ship of the Gods in the Alamber Sea. Only Geb's most faithful followers knew the location of this temple.

Activities
Geb's clergy within the Golden Forge spent the vast majority of their time attempting to stop the volcano from erupting, as it threatened to do since the Time of Troubles. Unbeknownst to the outside world, Geb's clergy saved the coasts of Unther from being buried by ash had the volcano erupted.

## Goodwife

Goodwives were specialty priestesses devoted to Nephthys, the Guardian of Wealth and Commerce within the Mulhorandi pantheon.

Activities
They tithed heavily to the Chuch of Nephthys, giving three quarters of all of their wealth to the church. In exchange for this the church took care of all of the funeral arrangements for members of the goodwife's family.

Culture
Goodwives were always human females of Mulhorandi descent who were either married or widowed. Goodwives despised Red Wizards from Thay.

Abilities
They could effectively calculate the value of an object simply by viewing it. They could cast invisibility to undead, protection from evil, negative plane protection, transmute metal to wood, holy word, and fire storm once per day. A glyph of warding could be cast three times per tenday. They were adept at casting spells from the wards sphere, enjoying faster casting times and extended duration on such spells. Goodwives never lost in a game of chance.

Possessions
Goodwives commonly carried a blunt weapon or perhaps a net or lasso. They did not have restrictions on the type of armor they could wear.

Description
Goblin Hunter had a delicate pearl handle, and its blade looked more like a hunting knife than a weapon.

Powers
The blade's enchantments were especially dangerous to goblins as its edge cut deeper into the creatures' hides. Functionally, the dagger became a +2 enchanted weapon when wielded against goblins.

History
Goblin Hunter was created sometime before the, by the enchantress Elytharra Dinnesmore, the wife of Ulbrec Dinnesmore, the speaker of Targos at the time. She created the magic blade as a way to defend herself from goblin raids that menaced the settlements of Icewind Dale. When asked about Goblin Hunter, Elytharra claimed that the weapon was used only a handful of times. As of 1312 DR, the enchantress was willing to part with Goblin Hunter for the right price.

In the, Elytharra's Goblin Hunter belonged to Graendel Granitefist of Graendel's Fine Dwarven Craft in Targos and was available for sale.

## Golden Dawn

Golden Dawn was the talking horse of Queen Zaranda Rhindaun of Tethyr in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Physically, "Goldie" was a standard medium-sized war horse. However, unlike a normal horse, Goldie was intelligent and could speak. While certainly magical, she was not a familiar, and it is not known how she came to use and understand language.

Goldie had a golden coat with a long white mane. Her ears were long and shapely for a mare, and had been described as similar in shape to the lateen sails of feluccas from Amn.

Personality

Goldie was a snarky, stubborn individual who was not afraid to speak her mind. She complained about being a riding horse, but in truth, she took great pride in her skill at carrying Zaranda.

Abilities
Besides her supernatural gift of speech, Golden Dawn was intelligent and fought strategically, giving warnings to her rider. Her large, triangular ears gave her the ability to hear crowds from at least a half mile (0.8 kilometers) away.

Possessions
Goldie was fitted with a Calimshite saddle that had a high cantle.

Unbeknownst to her caretakers, Goldie owned a set of loaded dice.

Activities
For entertainment, Goldie enjoyed playing dice with her grooms in the palace of Faerntarn. She cheated.

Relationships

Zaranda considered Goldie a true friend, and the horse called her mistress "Randi" as a term of endearment, though the human found the nickname too juvenile. Goldie could not actually read Zaranda's mind, but she knew the woman well enough that it sometimes appeared like she could. Usually, Zaranda admired Golden Dawn's council.

Goldie preferred to mate with stallions who were normal horses, unable to talk.

History
In the spring of the, Golden Dawn accompanied then-Countess and adventuress Zaranda Star on a journey from Thay to Zazesspur to sell loot.

Besides serving as Zaranda's mount in combat, Goldie once assisted her mistress in taking Castle Lutwill by pretending to be Zizzy, the Wonder Horse, companion of Fyadros, the Incomparably Wonderful Bard, so that Star Protective Services could be snuck into the castle at night.

Goldie remained Zaranda's riding mare after the latter was made queen.

## Godsfrown Shield

A Godsfrown Shield was a form of financial transaction that required payment in exchange for a future payment were some other condition to come to pass.

Use
The purchase of a Godsfrown Shield required the purchaser to make recurring payments to the seller. The shield was for a given physical thing, such as a house, ship, or cargo. Were it to be stolen, destroyed, or otherwise come to misfortune within the agreed terms, then the seller would instead pay out a drastically larger sum than the recurring payments, thus becoming the "Godsfrown Shield". Sellers of Godsfrown Shields were expected to make arrangements to prevent misfortune from coming to happen on the matters on which the Godsfrown Shield was purchased.

In short, according to sages of the Realms, it was a form of insurance.

History
Andemel Graeven purchased a Godsfrown Shield from Baerusin at some point prior to Ches 14 of the, securing his shop. Given Raurild Sarpath's comments on him having survived the fire, and his own later comments about there being a fire in his shop one hour from then, it was strongly implied he was going to commit insurance fraud.

Appendix
Background
The Godsfrown Shield figures in a section of Elminster in Hell told as a flashback. The memory is titled "Only a Small Sort of Dragon" in the Realmslore section of the book.

Appearances
Novels
Elminster in Hell

## Golden Vault

The Golden Vault was a secret organization dedicated to correcting moral wrongs and keeping dangerous magical artifacts away from ill-intentioned individuals. It specialized in cases that were ignored by official authorities.

Activities
In addition to performing delicate heists, operatives of the organization acted as spies, collecting information on future targets and looking for talented new recruits.

Base of Operations
The Golden Vault was rumored to originate from a good-aligned Outer Plane, but had operatives in major cities all over the multiverse.

Possessions
The Golden Vault provided their operatives with a magical music box. When opened using special golden keys, the music box would recite the goal of their next mission in a soothing voice. The golden key would then vanish once the box was closed.

Relationships
According to rumors, the Golden Vault was associated with well-intentioned metallic dragons. The organization would also associate with adventuring parties once they proved that they could be trusted.

History
At the request of Clan Axebreaker, the Golden Vault sent agents to Revel's End in order to retrieve a magical key that would allow them to retrieve stolen riches.

Appendix

Background
Within Keys from the Golden Vault, the titular Golden Vault is an optional method of introducing each adventure, but "Prisoner 13" and the other adventures conventionally run without it. The Golden Vault is included here for completeness, but is regarded as only adapted canon, that is, a non-canonical organization that may be adapted to the Realms.

Appearances
Adventures
 Keys from the Golden Vault: "Prisoner 13"

References

## Goblin stick

The goblin stick was a forked & hooked polearm developed by bugbears as both a weapon and a tool for capturing goblins that were hiding from the wielder.

Description
A goblin stick consisted of a wooden stick, measuring  in length, that was usually gripped in the middle. On each end, a goblin stick was tipped with three sharp, pointed blades. The center blade was curved into a hook, while those on the side were set off in different directions and at slight angles.

Weaponology
The hooked central blade of a goblin stick was used to pull a target out of tight spaces. The angled side blades of the weapon were used to search for targets around corners and to reach into places that were otherwise difficult to reach. Additionally, goblin sticks could be used to trip an opponent.

Notable Users
In addition to bugbears, goblin sticks were often made and utilized by hobgoblins and some ogres.

## Gohar

Gohar was a lone barkeep who ran the Salty Dog Tavern in the docks of Targos in the early 14 century DR.

Description
Gohar was a rotund human male. He resembled a large cast iron stove in the Salty Dog Tavern in size and continuous wheezing. He spoke with a sailor's drawl.

History
Gohar's youth was spent working as a mercenary. Sometime circa the, Gohar won a knife fight and ended up being the lucky owner of the Salty Dog Tavern. He did not intend running the business and wanted to sell the establishment and use the money on drinks and tars in Luskan. However, love changed Gohar's plans. He became enamored with the Salty Dog's previous owner's wife and they together ended up running the business for over a decade, until her death in the winter of the .

By the, Gohar was alone and married to the tavern. The drinking establishment remained open through the threat of the Legion of the Chimera and goblin sieges of Targos. When an orchestrated goblin attack took place, gaining them entrance to the town through magical means, Gohar's Salty Dog Tavern stayed active and served alcohol as the goblins ravaged the docks and warehouses. When mercenaries confronted Gohar, he refused to believe them that Targos was under attack as he did not hear a horn signal from the palisades nor screams calling folk to take arms.

## Gorg

Gorg was a hobgoblin prisoner at the Revel's End prison on the Frozenfar in the late 1490s DR.

Description
Standing some, Gorg had pointed ears, jagged teeth, and long black hair, and was muscular and heavily scarred.

History

For unspecified crimes, Gorg was transported to the Revel's End prison on the coast of the Sea of Moving Ice. On the sleigh ride there, however, even whilst manacled hand and foot, he killed his fellow prisoner by twisting his neck, then alleged it had been a bumpy ride to the guards. Four had to escort him, including Lucas, and they shoved Gorg into a cell with Holga Kilgore and Edgin Darvis—despite it being only a two-person cell. But when he sleazed onto Holga and made a grab for her, she beat him up, breaking both his legs and knocking him out cold on the frozen waste bucket.Short of an overcrowded prison population, it seems that the guards expected for Holga to beat Gorg up for them.

One day, a young duergar by the name of Shull reached out to the Godfather, eager to develop his psionic abilities. The Godfather directed the power-hungry youth to seek out the mind flayer known as Ir, far from their home city.

Some time later, Shull returned to the Godfather, armed with the ability to destroy a single mind with a mere command word. Eager to kill the Godfather and seize his power for himself, Shull demonstrated his newfound ability, and immediately exploded his own skull. ## Gorget (disambiguation)

## Gorget

Gorgets were a common form of armor designed to protect the neck and throat from attacks, such as strangulation, though were also worn by some ornamentally.

Description

A gorget consisted of two semicircular metal plates that overlapped, encircling the wearer's neck, and were held in place with either a metal pin or leather straps. Some gorgets had spikes added to them for offense or intimidation, while others meant for ornamentation might be inset with gemstones.

Gorgets were commonly worn as part of a set of full plate, either alone or as part of a helmet. They were often paired with great helms in particular, in order to offset the weight that style of helmet put upon its wearer.

Availability
 Haerldoun's Helms and Shields, an armor shop in Wheloon, sold gorgets.
 Hasklar's Arms & Armor, an armor shop in Yartar, sold gorgets with small interior pouches for 4 gold pieces.
 The Iron Hand, a smithy in Eveningstar.

History
The existence of gorgets on Toril dated as far back as the Netherese Empire. Ornamental gorgets began being popular among Netheril's upperclass during the nation's Golden Age.

By the 1370's DR, gorgets were commonly issued in nations across the Heartlands to various nobles, knights, cavalrymen and officers for wear alongside great helms and plate mail. Around this time, new trade routes in the Upperdark allowed for dwarves to sell their wares, such as gorgets, in human cities across the Heartlands.

Varieties of Gorgets
Double-thickness gorget: a variety of gorget developed by Imroad Gaskulyn that concealed a thin storage compartment between its two layers.

Notable Gorgets
 Battle gorget, a variety of enchanted gorgets that were developed by Manshoon.
 Gorget of the Songdragon, a gorget that carried an enchantment to shield against Ahghairon's dragonward.

Notable Wearers
Individuals
 Aosinin Truesilver.
 Ismur Tamrathdreir, a Turmishan wizard, owned an enchanted gorget that allowed the wearer to cast blink and dimension door.
 Manshoon, founder of the Zhentarim, always wore his creation the battle gorget.
 Rhamlaen Mamarrath, a noble in Thentia, sometimes wore ornamental gorgets at parties.
 Sharantyr.
 Tordon Sureblade, the Lord Magistrate of Ravens Bluff, commonly wore a gorget that had protected him from strangulation attempts on three separate occasions.
 Yamun Khahan wore a gorget of hammered steel.
Nations, Regions, & Settlements
 Gorgets could be found in use by armed forces in the Blade Kingdoms.
 In the Netherese Empire, gorgets were common among both the commoners of Low Netheril and the guards of the floating Netherese enclaves. Ornamental gorgets beset with gems, often enchanted with protective effects, were popular among the upperclass, typically being worn by arcanists and the archwizards of enclaves. Some arcanists even enchanted their gorgets with a variety of different offensive effects.
 Around the mid-14 century DR, men in the city of Waterdeep commonly wore silver gorgets inset with a spiral of rubies.
Organizations
 The City Guard of Waterdeep would dress down to gorgets during the summer season.
 The City Watch of Silverymoon typically wore gorgets.
 All members of the Vigilant, the law enforcement of Tarmalune, wore black gorgets regardless of their rank.
Races & Sentient Creatures
 Some gold dwarves were known to wear gorgets, typically made of gold.
Religions
 The general clergy of the Church of Bane all wore black gorgets with one of more instances of Bane's symbol painted on in red to denote rank.
 The general clergy of Vergadain wore gorgets bearing their god's symbol alongside chainmail.

## Gout

Gout was a disease that caused pain. An individual suffering from gout could be said to be "gouty".

Acquisition
Gout was thought to be caused by overeating.

Effects
Gout caused great discomfort and pain, particularly in a sufferer's big toe. However, it had its origin in other parts of the body, even if the sufferer only noticed the pain in one place; this was an aspect the great physician-priest Odum spoke of. Hence, a physician would need to examine a patient's whole body, naked if need be.While this is true of gout, Benelaius is speaking generally and possibly exaggerating as part of a ruse to get Tobald to remove his clothes. As such, a Faerûnian physician's knowledge of gout remains uncertain. It could cause a limp if it affected the toes.

If untreated or improperly treated, gout would progress and worsen over time.

Treatment
Sufferers of gout might be recommended to modify their diet, such as by eating less and particularly by consuming less ale and red meat.

Frankincense was generally thought to be useful for curing gout. Autumn crocus was used in the Western Heartlands to make a tincture to treat the condition, and in the Eastern Heartlands, pills and salves might be used to treat it, including salves made from the dung of snakes. Medications to blunt the disease's symptoms could take a day for a skilled healer to make.

History
In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, Mayor Tobald of Ghars, Cormyr, suffered from gout—notably in his big toe—despite the efforts of the local doctor, Braum, and the wizard Benelaius. Braum advised Tobald to eat less, but the mayor complained this was an absurd thing for a physician to say. Braum also prescribed snake dung salve, of which Tobald was equally scathing.

In the late 15 century DR, a blacksmith named Sampson from Moonhaven suffered from gout.

Powers
This staff's ends were constantly ablaze with flames, seemingly fueled by air and leaving no scorch damage on the weapon itself. In addition to the fire damage the weapon dealt, it possessed a simple +1 enchantment that guided it toward the target and boosted its damage even further.

In the, one such staff belonged to Roah Moonglow, a hin agent of the Zhentarim who did business with the recently emerged Cult of the Absolute in the Western Heartlands of Faerûn.

## Golden Fortress

The Golden Fortress was a large fortified military base and academy in Eshpurta that housed and trained the Council Army of Amn.

Description
The academy was made up a large central structure with a number of smaller outbuildings. A large wall surrounded the entire complex, running down to the banks of the nearby river.

Geography
The Golden Fortress was located just outside of Eshpurta next to the Eshpurta Road, on the opposite side of Trifin Creek as it joined the River Rimril.

Activities
The Golden Fortress was the primary training ground for the Council Army's recruits, and the command center for all military operations in eastern Amn. The wide plains between the Troll Mountains and Snowflake Mountains were considered a likely route for outside invasions, and so Eshpurta was turned into a heavily militarized garrison city.

Befitting its martial inhabitants, the Fortress contained chapels to Helm, Tyr, Torm, and Tempus. During the mid-to-late 14 century DR they were all overseen by Shield Brother Benitus, a venerable priest of Helm assisted by younger priests of many faiths.

Defenses
Between the Golden Fortress and Eshpurta, the local Council Army forces were made up of 18,000 soldiers and 6,300 officers, as well as 3,000 trainees and 700 military instructors, craftsmen, and other support staff. They were joined by the 2,000 Shield Militia of the city.

The officers of the Fortress kept an eye on predatory businesses that could harm the welfare of their soldiers in Eshpurta. If a particular enterprise in the city such as a loan-shark or gambling den proved exceptionally troublesome, the problem was usually resolved by an unfortunate accident during catapult drills that destroyed the establishment.

Inhabitants
 Commander Balacer Macefist.
 Shield Brother Benitus.
 Ard Roton, Swanmantle of the Heralds of Faerûn in Eshpurta.

## Gohumberry Tresp

Gohumberry Tresp was an inhabitant of the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, the Cult of the Absolute infiltrated the walls of Baldur's Gate whilst allied with the cult of Bhaal. Orin the Red, the crazed Chosen of the Lord of Murder, and her doppelgangers performed ritualistic murders of Baldurians, using as a base their so-called Tribunal, deep under the Lower City. Gohumberry Tresp grew suspicious of the rising stench of death that emanated from the City Sewers' drains and hatches. Gohumberry began investigating the scandalous issue and petitioned the Department of Drains and Underways for a resolution to the problem. The Department claimed to be handling the problem and sternly warned Gohumberry away from attempting to enter the Underways on his own. Continuing his investigation regardless, Gohumberry found an informant who'd discovered information on the so-called Tribunal. This, of course, attracted the attention of the Bhaalists. Before the man was able to get his hands on the informant's intel, Orin personally tracked him down to the baths near the Basilisk Gate. Gohumberry was slaughtered and beheaded; his body and innards were used to decorate the baths in an unholy and savage manner.

## Golden lute of Shazar

Golden lutes of Shazar were a unique lutes that were enchanted by the music-loving aristocrat from Mulhorand named Shazar sometime before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
Apart from dramatically improving the owner's musical aptitude, at least one of the golden lutes of Shazar allows the bard wielder to cast several spells. The cure serious wounds spell cast at a high proficiency level could be dispensed once per day, and the same went for a powerful version of a haste spell.

History
Ten golden lutes of Shazar were commissioned by Shazar long before the, to serve as a gift for the pharaoh of the time. The pharaoh, a creature of musical taste and thunderous temper, was delighted hearing the ten golden lutes sing in the hands of master minstrels. Unbeknownst to the pharaoh, skill-busting enchantments were weaved into the instruments, allowing the minstrels to perform beyond their natural skills. Eventually, the ruler found out about the magic and took it as a great offense. Shazar and his minstrels were executed. Despite the incident, the pharaoh kept all ten lutes, and after his death, they were interred along with the pharaoh.

Many years later, the pharaoh's tomb was plundered, and the ten golden lutes of Shazar were scattered across Faerûn. In 1374 DR, one of the lutes belonged to a musician Cain Lethellon, who cockily challenged bards and entertainers to a lute-off in the Blacklake District of Neverwinter. Some said that the minstrel was beaten in his own competition by the famed Kalach-Cha or one of their companions, losing the Golden lutes of Shazar as a result.

## Golmuth

Golmuth, the Fifth City of the Sword, was one of the core cities in ancient Jhaamdath.

Geography
Golmuth was a few days west of the First City, Naarkolyth, just north of Gharrent and south of the Orsraun Mountains. After its destruction, it lay in the Selmal Basin beneath the waters of the Vilhon Reach.

History
Golmuth was founded in  as the fifth of Jhaamdath's cities, preceded the same year by nearby Gharrent.

In the, Jhaamdath began aggressively deforesting the Chondalwood and slaying the elves of Nikerymath. The elven response in the  was a High Magic ritual that caused a great wave from the Inner Sea to wash over the Cities of the Sword and destroy the aggressors. Golmuth remained a sunken ruin hidden beneath the water, with its many lost psionic artifacts slowly affecting the nearby environment.

The sunken ruins of Jhaamdath were suddenly revealed by the lower sea levels that followed the Spellplague in 1385 DR, becoming the Vilhon Wilds. They were then returned to obscurity by the Great Rain and Second Sundering in 1486 DR.

## Gothaladreum

Gothaladreum was an ancient cemetery once used by the folk of Port Llast, located on the edge of the Neverwinter Wood, long abandoned and ruined by the late 14 century DR.

 Description
By the late 14 century DR, Gothaladreum was an old and weathered ruin with most of its gravestones and monuments overgrown, mausoleums crumbled, and the entire cemetery was wrapped in eery silence. Gothaladreum was connected to the Trade Way via a small path that led east.

History

Gothaladreum became the final resting place for Lord Adreum VI of Port Llast sometime before the late 1000s DR. His grave stood just to the east of the cemetery's entrance, decorated with a statue of a winged angelic being. However, despite being interred, Lord Adreum VI did not find peace. His oath to protect Port Llast was unfulfilled, and for the centuries that followed, when the town was in danger, Adreum rose from his ancient grave as a restless ghost. In the years that followed the Shadow War of the, Port Llast's neighbor – the city of Luskan saw an opportunity in a weakened state of Neverwinter and its economy still recovering from the two recent wars. Luskan sent a force to claim the port city. This prompted the undead of Gothaladreum to rise and walk the streets of Port Llast every night.

When a band of adventurers became involved with the invasion and the walking dead, they ventured into Gothaladreum and confronted the ancient restless Lord. History books did not record how the conflict was resolved. Some claimed that the adventurers sided with Luskan and destroyed the undead, while others said the heroes asked Lord Adreum VI to fulfill his oath to defend Port Llast from the Luskanite invaders. However, the walking dead of Gothaladreume risen by the necromancer named Nya, who were disturbing the burial sites to drive the Luskanites out. In order to get Lord Adreum VI and his undead servants' help, the adventurers needed to kill the necromancer or convincer her to step down.

## Gomen

Gomen was a village located in Miyama Province in Kozakura.

Description
Gomen, the Tara-gawa Basin's largest fishing village, was situated on terraced slopes by the shore of Miumi. It featured a harbor capable of accommodating ocean-going vessels. The village was a hive of activity for the region, with three hundred buildings, predominantly minka and godowns.

Trade
Fishing and boat-building were the cornerstones of Gomen's economy. Fishermen engaged in the lucrative trade of drying and salting the daily catch for export. The village's shipwrights were esteemed for their craftsmanship, frequently commissioned by wealthy merchants and local lords to construct robust sea vessels.

Beyond these primary industries, Gomen boasted a diverse range of facilities: two smithies, an armorer, a sake brewery, two inns, a gambling house, two temples, a shrine, a weaving establishment, and several carpentry workshops. Additionally, a local merchant operated a modest shipping business with a warehouse and three ships, primarily transporting agricultural produce to Tamanokuni and Kuda.

Government
Strategically positioned at the junction of two shoen, one owned by the Hori clan and the other by the Tsu family, Gomen became a focal point of interest for both families, owing to its significant economic potential. However, the village recently acquired a special charter from the shugo-daimyo, which exempted it from the jurisdiction of these estates in exchange for direct tax payments to the shugo-daimyo, placing Gomen under the Niwa family's administration.

As of, the village was overseen by the samurai Matsue Fusu.

Defenses
A small defensive contingent comprising five samurai and fifteen bushi ensured Gomen's safety and kept order.

## Gobo (village)

Gobo was a village located in Miyama Province in Kozakura.

Description
Gobo was a quaint and serene community of seventy-eight buildings, and was renowned for its picturesque and captivating landscape.

Trade
This charming setting made the village a favored destination for travelers, poets, artists, and nobility. To cater to these visitors, Gobo boasted a well-regarded inn and provided various services tailored to the needs of its distinguished guests. Besides its appeal to visitors, the village also sustained an active fishing industry and operated a mill.

Government
As of, the village's jito was Fukada Shiro. He oversaw the shoen encompassing the village.

Defenses
Fukada Shiro maintained a significant military presence of thirty-six samurai and sixty bushi.

## Golden Goblet Inn

The Golden Goblet Inn was a lively and festive tavern and inn in the town of Mossbridges, established in the late 14 century DR.

Location
The inn stood on the main street in Mossbridges. Its main entrance was easily noticeable thanks to a sign depicting a goblet hanging over the inn's door.

Atmosphere
The town of Mossbridges was a relatively sleepy place, making the Golden Goblet Inn feel slightly out of place due to its festive appearance and bustling, lively atmosphere.

Services
The Goblet had nothing unique on offer. It had rental lodgings priced at a single gold piece per night in a shared or private room for two gold pieces. Private room guests were offered complementary morningfeast. The food and drink available were humble but cheap. Alcohol included ale for a single silver per pitcher, mead – five silvers, beer – five copper pieces, and table wine – two silvers. Premium drinks included elven mead or dwarven spirits for a single gold, and exotic Thayan bloodwine for five gold pieces. The inn also offered juice for one silver.

Food menu consisted of single-dish meals: a platter of roast beast with potatoes for two gold coins, roast pork with roasted vegetables for three gold, fried chicken garnished with sliced apples for two golds, a dish called Vegetable Melody for five silvers, baked flounder – one gold, and lastly, the most expensive platter – stuffed devil crab for five gold. Selection of sweet treats was limited to cheese cake or coffee cake sold for a silver, bread and cheese platter for one silver, or exotic vanilla-flavored ice cream from the famous Resort.

History
The Golden Goblet Inn sprouted during Mossbridges' prosperous time of growth and expansion that followed The Three Women War in the Vast and its alliance with the neighboring metropolis of Ravens Bluff.

Inhabitants
In the late 14 century DR, the inn was run by Georgio, its kindly human proprietor, who was usually found behind the bar, serving drinks or trading stories at guests' tables. Astrala was a waitress and hostess who worked for Georgio during that time. The petite bard named Shelaya entertained the inn's guests with a wide variety of songs and tales.

Description
Like his siblings, Golin had short-cropped brown hair and brown eyes.

Relationships
Golin had two brothers, Borin, and the youngest among the three, Chad. The brothers were acquainted with the retired half-orc fighter named Chester Hearth, who frequented the Golden Goblet Inn in Mossbridges. They considered the fighter to be a talented trapper and thought him to be a fine fellow despite being a half-orc. At one point, the siblings' dog was denied breeding with Chester's powerful moorhound named Suzzane.

History
When the Golden Goblet Inn was established in the late 14 century DR, after The Three Women War, Golin and his brothers became frequent patrons of Georgio's fine establishment. The family celebrated Chad's 20 birthday at the Goblet.

## Goddard

Goddard was a Netherese priest who served as an Underchief at Constabulary Branch Three in Eileanar in Netheril before its destruction in .

Activities
Goddard helped manage the constables of the branch alongside Underchiefs Morian and Cadogen for Chief Constable Emblemat. Despite the harsh conditions she loved her job, and was completely loyal to Emblemat. She was especially adept at engendering respect from her underlings, with whom she was always available to talk and give advice to. Although she herself admitted she didn't have all the answers, she always did her best to help and would point the supplicant in the right direction to get the best possible advice.

## Gorcraw

The gorcraw was a kind of carrion-eating bird.

Description
These birds appeared as crosses between crows and turkey vultures or buzzards. They were usually black, but could be purple-blue, and young gorcraws could be mottled white, beige, or even brown, often blending them with forest detritus. Most gorcraws, about the size of a sparrow, could only squawk.

Behavior
Gorcraws were rapacious, but slow and slothful. Though not very smart, they were not stupid, either: they were impatient if anything, unlikely to wait for prey to die, preferring to fly off and seek it elsewhere. These animals were known to eat young of the same species.

Gorcraws were known to love the taste of blood and sarsson mixed together.

Abilities
According to people familiar with the matter, a gorcraw was close enough to a dire crow.

Combat
Gorcraws hunted, like shrikes did, when they were desperate, but they were not agile fliers and made for poor birds of prey. They generally avoided any creature larger than themselves or humans, as they were nearly defenseless against farmers, or even children. They didn't get close around anyone with a large stick, recognizing those as sufficient to injure or kill them.

Ecology
Gorcraws were carrion birds, and occasionally hunters.

History
The word "gorcraw" originated in Sembia, and may have been occasionally mixed up with the close-sounding gorscraw. Some reported the name as deriving from its appearance being so close to that of a "noisy black crow".

In the 14 century DR, gorcraws lived in Marsember, and in the Storm Horns.

Appendix
Background
The gorcraw is likely named for the carrion crow, a type of crow, which is known in some parts of England as a 'gorecrow' or 'gorcrow'.

Appearances

References

---

## Golbraith Stredivas

Golbraith Stredivas was a human wizard, a retired adventurer, a mind flayer hunter, and the author of Confidant of a Duke as well as On Psionic Manipulations and Countermeasures, who resided in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Golbraith was a man with curly hair and trimmed beard silvered with age. His skin had deep wrinkles and the scars of a life of danger. His blue eyes were framed by a monocle, and his aged body was draped in mage's robes. He had a habit of talking to himself, which developed in his late life's solitude.

Abilities
Golbraith Stredivas was an experienced war-caster wizard who was proficient in spell-sculpting.

Relationships
Golbraith was renowned as an expert on the topic of mind flayers and a master of counter-illithid measures. His fame was respected even at the citadel library of Candlekeep. At the time of the Absolute crisis, the First Reader of Candlekeep sent a letter to Golbraith requesting his expertise. The adventurer also had dealings with the Society of Brilliance and The Lodge in the Lower City as well as Havkelaag' one of its researchers who used Golbraith's address to receive mail. His work on anti-illithid countermeasures received some acclaim among adventurers, and some sent the author letters of gratitude and recounts of how the work saved their lives.

History
In his youth, Golbraith made a living as an adventurer and a monster hunter. The wizard frequented the Elfsong Tavern in the Lower City when he was within Baldur's Gate's walls. During one of his ventures to "feel the pulse of the city," the Grand Duke of the time, the famed Bhaalspawn Abdel Adrian, met Golbraith at one of the terrace tables of the Elfsong. The two accidentally bought each other's drinks by calling the same waiter to them. The strangers stroke up a conversation, and when the Duke found out that Golbraith was a fellow adventurer and a monster hunter, he showered Stredivas with questions. At the next meeting, the Duke revealed that he, too, was an adventurer once, and afterward, they met regularly at the same tavern, turning conversations into a friendship. Many tendays later, Golbraith realized that his new friend was the Grand Duke. Sometime before the Grand Duke's death, he gifted Abdel's trusted shield to his friend.

When the Absolute menaced Baldur's Gate in the, Golbraith was already an aged and retired man, forced to abandon the life of daring adventure after suffering a devious illiquid trap. Despite that, he was confident in the city's safety, knowing that his son fought for the Gate and for its people.

Relationships
Sometime in his younger days, Golbraith fathered a son, who by the, was twice as great a fighter as Golbraith was and was also a capable illithid slayer, who lived away from Golbraith's large home in the Lower City.

Works
He also penned a book about his friendship with Abdel Adrian, Confidant of a Duke, as well as a guide to protecting oneself from mind flayers, On Psionic Manipulations and Countermeasures, sometime before 1492 DR.

## Goldmane's Dazzler

Goldmane's Dazzler was a magical lantern.

Description
Goldmane's Dazzler was an exquisitely crafted artifact, appearing as a lion's head meticulously carved with eyes of large green gemstones, affixed to a cylindrical base of gold. The materials employed in its construction were of substantial value, amounting to thousands of gold pieces. A handle, composed of gold with an ivory grip, extended from the lion's ears. Unique among lanterns, Goldmane's Dazzler emitted light from its open jaws without the necessity for any combustible fuel. This luminosity was perpetual and could not be extinguished once the jaws were parted. Inscribed on the reverse of the gold cylinder were the words "Dazzle" and "Roar," rendered in the Common script.

Abilities
When the jaws were opened and the command "Dazzle" was spoken, a beam of dazzling light emanated from the lion's mouth. This beam functioned similarly to the spell confusion. This ability was ineffective against creatures that were blind and could only be activated once every 24 hours.

When the command "Roar" was spoken and the jaws were opened, several effects occurred simultaneously. Firstly, the light dimmed, and the eyes of the lion glowed intensely. Secondly, there was a significant inrush of air, which extinguished all torches within . Immediately following this, the lion's head emitted a powerful roar. This roar deafened all creatures within  and generated a cone of force that extended  in length and  in width at the end. Any creature within this cone suffered damage and was knocked off its feet. The cone of force also destroyed doors and light wooden structures less than  thick, and inflicted structural damage to stone or earth structures. If used underground, it had the potential to cause a cave-in. This powerful effect could only be utilized once per week.

History
Goldmane's Dazzler was crafted in the city of Myth Drannor at the height of its cultural prominence. It was the work of a skilled dwarven goldsmith and was enchanted by the wizard Goldmane, designed to be both a masterpiece of beauty and a potent magical artifact. When Myth Drannor fell, the lantern was taken by an elf mage named Silverhair, who intended to transport it to Evermeet. However, Silverhair's party was ambushed by a band of orcs. Utilizing surprise and superior numbers, the orcs overpowered the elves and enslaved them. The lantern remained in the hall of the orc chieftain until it was stolen by a group of halfling thieves known as Swifthand, Inc., who infiltrated the lair and absconded with a significant amount of treasure, including the lantern.

The halfling band sold the lantern to a trader on the Wyvernwater in Cormyr, who subsequently sold it to a wealthy merchant from Arabel. The merchant kept the lantern for several years until his death, at which point he bequeathed it to a favored nephew residing in Scornubel. Unfortunately, the caravan transporting the lantern to the nephew was attacked by bandits just west of High Horn. The merchant's nephew offered a substantial reward for its return.

## Goblin Wars (1358 DR)

The Goblin Wars were a series of conflicts fought in the kingdom of Erlkazar against invading goblins in 1358 DR.

History
Early in the, King Ertyk Uhl of the Starrock tribe led a horde of goblins down from the High Peaks and the Kuldin Peaks to invade Erlkazar, then under the rule of King Valon Morkann. For over three months, the goblins laid siege to the city of Duhlnarim. The goblins' numbers appeared limitless and many heroes and Duhlnarim's mages failed to stop them.

Finally, however, Elestam's Crusaders forged an alliance with the Shieldbreakers, a reliable ogre mercenary force from Rivenshield. The war turned and the goblins were cut down to under a third of their number. After almost a full year of fighting, the conflict ended before the close of the year.

Legacy
During the conflict, a brewer of Llorbauth, Araneth Idogyr, met and befriended a cyclops, from whom he acquired the recipe for Old One Eye ale.

Erlkazar and the Shieldbreakers maintained their alliance against the High Peaks goblins and giants as of 1367 DR.

According to reports, the goblins had just begun to regain their strength a decade later.

Combatants
Casualties
Among the casualties was Rysodyl Boughstrong, the elf husband of High Watcher Laxaella Bronshield, Baroness of Tanistan. Rysodyl was killed by King Ertyk Uhl himself.

## Goblin Wars (disambiguation)

Goblin War or Goblin Wars could refer to:
 The perpetual goblin wars that led to the fall of the dwarf kingdom of Xonathanur;
 The Goblin Wars that led to the destruction of the city of Tor Mak;
 The Goblin Wars (200 DR) fought in Cormyr between the kingdom's defenders and the goblinkin of Hlundadim around 200 DR;
 The Goblin Wars fought in the North between the Uthgardt, the Covenant, and the goblins of the Valley of Khedrun in 753 DR;
 The Goblin Wars (1358 DR) fought in Erlkazar between the kingdom's defenders, the Shieldbreakers ogres, and the Starrock goblin tribe in 1358 DR; or
 The Goblin War fought in Cormyr between the kingdom's defenders and a force of Grodd goblins, orcs, and ghazneths under the dragon Nalavarauthatoryl over 1370 to 1371 DR.

## Goblin Wars (200 DR)

The Goblin Wars were a series of conflicts fought in the kingdom of Cormyr against the goblins of Hlundadim around 200 DR.

History
Battle
Directed by the mysterious mage known only as Great Hlundadim, the goblinkin of Hlundadim invaded Cormyr in the . They razed the settlement of Arabel and continued on into the King's Forest. Battle after battle would follow.

Finally, north of Suzail in the, King Moriann Obarskyr led the armies of Cormyr into battle against the Hlundadim goblins. The goblins were defeated and forced into the Stonelands.

Aftermath
Victory was a costly one for Cormyr: Moriann's two sons and heirs were killed in the fighting, and the king himself died the following year whilst at a feast celebrating the victory. He was succeeded by his brother, Tharyann the Elder.

The knight Varanth, "Thundersword", a loyal companion of King Moriann, was one of the first deaths of the war, and was followed into the grave by five of his sons. The sixth and surviving son was a popular young knight named Rayn, who claimed inheritance of his father's sword, Symylazarr, which had been taken up by Moriann during the war and was almost buried with the king. Tharyann, not yet even crowned, agreed so as not to anger the people. A tenday later, Rayn returned and pledged his fealty to the now King Tharyann, laying Symylazarr at his feet. The king ennobled Rayn as the first Lord Thundersword, while Symylazarr was adopted by the Obarskyrs for knighting and oaths of fealty as Moriann had used it during the war.

During the conflict, the Alsevir family was also raised to the nobility.

Hlundadim collapsed under the expansion of the Anauroch desert in the .

## Goblin Wars

The Goblin Wars were a series of conflicts fought in the North between the goblins of the Valley of Khedrun, the Uthgardt Alliance, and the Covenant in 753 DR.

History
Causes
In the, the mages of the Covenant began to covertly and magically manipulate the various Uthgardt tribes. By the end of the season, all the tribes stood united and ready to battle the goblinoids of the Savage Frontier, in what would be known as the Uthgardt Alliance.

These Uthgardt were tasked, in the, with hunting down and killing as many orc chieftains as possible. Twenty were eliminated over the next five years, preventing the formation of a horde.

War
Then, in the, a horde of goblins burst out of the Valley of Khedrun in the Spine of the World mountains and marched southward. They quickly overcame the defenders of Mirabar and looted the city. A goblin chieftain named Nethaug raided the tomb of Prince Ereskas Torlath, founder of Mirabar, and stole the sword Namarra.

Several of the Uthgardt tribes fought the goblins fiercely and relentlessly, facing them day and night for almost all season. Their efforts reduced the goblins' numbers significantly.

Finally, the Covenant led the people of the Dessarin Valley against the goblins and wiped them out.

Aftermath
Following the battle, goblins were rendered nearly extinct in the North. However, Nethaug, with Namarra in hand, and others escaped back into the Spine of the World, into a ravine so remote that they would hold it securely well into the 14 century DR.

After the Goblin Wars, the Uthgardt Alliance battled another army of goblins and orcs led by ogres that marched out of the Evermoors in the . The loss of the Uthgardt warriors and the suffering of the tribes, with the Elk tribe almost going extinct, led to the dissolution of the Alliance by .
