## Goblet of Shadows

The Goblet of Shadows was a tavern in Nethra during the mid–8 century DR.

Interior
The place was a maze of arches, beaded curtains, and tables. There were also pillars.

Atmosphere
The Goblet was a shady place, where bar fights drew not just bets, but thrown bottles from strangers, and death screams did not disrupt activities, instead drawing applause. Bizarrely, it was not even the wildest tavern in the city.

Services
The place served alcohol, including heartsfire, and had dancing ladies.

History
One evening in the late,Per the Google Books version of Temptation, its realmslore section declares the events of the first part to happen in around five years starting 759 DR. In the first chapter, Nuressa, the warrior, tells Elminster they're in early Mirtul. If it is assumed that the events happened in at most five and a half years, as more than five and a half years becomes around six years, then the last events in part 1 must happen at most during Uktar of 764. Given Feldrin's warning during the coronation that the Lords of Laothkund are already planning an attack, this timeline seems plausible. Further development may yet alter the result. shortly before the invasion of Nethra by the Lords of Laothkund, the guards Delver and Ingrath were approached by Queen Dasumia of Galadorna, who was at the moment posing as one of the tavern dancers. She revealed her identity to the guards and proposed they go somewhere more private. This was witnessed by the scholars Tabarast and Beldrune, who were drinking at the time; the trio was also followed by the wandering elven mage Ilbryn Starym.

## Goblet of glory

A goblet of glory was an enchanted golden goblet that bestowed different effects upon those who imbibed from it depending upon how experienced of adventurer they were.

Powers
Once per day, whenever the rim of a goblet of glory was brought up to a holder's lips and tilted it would dispense a clear, glowing liquid. It was impossible for this liquid to be spilled out or extracted in any other way than drinking it, with the goblet otherwise appearing empty. Each goblet of glory could provide around 3 to 36 draughts. Upon reaching their limit, a goblet would crumble into dust.

For an unremarkable adventurer this liquid would permanently heal minor wounds, cause them to physically grow  taller, and temporarily bestow an increase in life energy akin to a potion of super-heroism, thus boosting their combat prowess. For an adventurer that was already exceptional, this liquid would merely provide a permanent healing of minor wounds and drain away some of their life energy. These effects lasted for a duration of 4 to 16 minutes.

History
In the, as the Absolute crisis ravaged the Gate, Gowlan's fishing business was damaged further by pollution of the Gray Harbor. Fishers in the Lower City kept pulling up nets filled with fish covered in black oily goo. Not just his catch, but also Fleer's and Gowlan's boat was soiled by the oil, leaving him frustrated and unable to lean the gunk off. To make things worse, Fleer and Gowlan fished a dead waveservant from the Gray Harbor. In reality, the fish were smothered in oil pollution left behind by a submersible used by the Cult of Bane to transport Lord Gortash's prisoners into the drowned Iron Throne headquarters prison at the bottom of Gray Harbor.

## Goggles

Goggles were a kind of headwear that covered the eyes in a practical and protective manner. A related form were snow goggles, which protected the wearer's eyes from blindingly bright snow.

Description
Goggles resembled spectacles, though with a much tighter fit that enclosed the eyes completely. The lenses were usually cut from glass and the headband made of a leather or heavy-duty cloth.

Goggles protected the wearer's eyes from all sorts of hazards, such as flying insects, noxious gases, dust, high-speed winds, and many other airborne hazards. The lenses could even be of special concave or convex designs that functioned as spectacles for a short- or long-sighted wearer.

In addition to their other properties, magical goggles were usually made to resize to fit widely ranging head sizes, allowing most people to easily wear them provided they had a human-like skull shape.

Notable Goggles
 Goggles of astral seeing, which improved vision in the Astral Plane.
 Goggles of minute seeing, a helmet that included magnifying lenses.
 Goggles of night, which granted the wearer a form of darkvision.
 Malchor's undersun goggles, which greatly improved low-light vision in the Underdark.
 Knight Vision Goggles, which made the wearer look like a knight.
 Shadowsight, a Shadovar device that saw partway into the Shadowfell.

## Goreball

Goreball was a dangerous competitive team sport played in Faerûn.

Description
Goreball was played between two teams, each of which had eight members. The central piece of the game was a gibbering mouther that was shaped into a ball. The playing field was a long and narrow grass-covered pitch, which had lines at each end representing a team's goal. The aim of the game was to maneuver the mouther across the opposing goal line. The first team to reach 13 goals won the game. A game of goreball could also result in a tie, which resulted in 'Endless Sudden Death.' Under this rule, the game continued until a final point was scored or none of the players could play anymore. The sport was made difficult due to the mouther's spittle and gibbering.

The game had a number of different positions, each associated with different tactics:
 'Wranglers' were tasked with moving the mouther towards the goal.
 'Smashers' acted aggressively to push other players around and towards the mouther.
 'Shield-bearers' defended their team against the dangerous mouther.
 Each team also had one 'Fresh Meat,' who lured the mouther towards the goal themselves. This position was the most desired but also the most dangerous.

Important to the play of goreball were formations, which were planned out in advance and had names. One formation, the 'Statue of Cyric' maneuver, was popularized by the Luskan Giants team. It involved using the spell polymorph to transform a player into something fearsome to distract the opposing team. The 'tornado' formation was a flying diversionary tactic best used by dragon players.
Players typically wore colored spiked jerkins, while earmuffs were banned items.

Notable Participants
The Dungeon Academy Flumphs was a goreball team that represented the Dungeon Academy. Their rivals were the more successful Waterdeep Dragons. Another team was the Luskan Giants.
 Kifin Stormclash was the minotaur coach who trained the Dungeon Academy Flumphs.
 A slender green dragon was the coach for the Waterdeep Dragons.
 Hugo and Snabla of the Danger Club were fans of goreball, as was their friend Tavian Truescale.

History
Goreball was played as part of the Tourney of Terror, a grand competition between the Dungeon Academy Flumphs and the Waterdeep Dragons. It had been held every 50 years, for about 300 years. In one match, 150 years prior to the Tourney of Terror that the Danger Club attended, a period of Endless Sudden Death continued for nearly three days.

## Golden Hills Hearth

The Golden Hills Hearth was a festhall in the underground city-state of Blingdenstone in the 14 and 15 centuries DR.

Structure
Rather than a building proper, the Golden Hills Hearth was housed in a sprawling burrow, with an entry door that was sized for those of gnomish height.

Services
This festhall catered largely to the young, unattached men of svirfneblin society. In addition to favors for coin, the festhall also functioned as a tavern, theater, and casino. Their services were rated by travelers as being excellent and moderately priced, offering up quite the cozy environment.

Any large surface dwellers that came into the establishment would usually find themselves shooed off by the proprietor and redirected to Blingdenstone's local alehouse, the Foaming Mug.

Inhabitants
In the late 15 century DR, the mistress of the festhall was one Splitshale. The coin-lasses who worked at this festhall went by the collective title "Ladies of the Golden Hills", which was in reference to the fabled lost goddesses of the Gnome pantheon.

## Good mantle

The Good mantle was a psionic mantle that granted ardents and divine minds an array of psionics powers and abilities derived from the universal concept of good.

Deities

Abilities
Manifesters of this mantle gained the unique psionic ability to expend their psionic focus to cause greater harm to any creature that wasn't good-aligned with their next attack using a melee weapon, ranged weapon, or spell. Additionally, the psychic aura exuded by divine minds of this mantle made all weapons of nearby allies strike as though they were good-aligned weaponry, allowing them to overcome alignment-based resistances.

Powers
Manifesters were able to guard against evil creatures with psionic protection from evil, take on the burdens of others with empathic transfer, and temporarily make themselves celestial with planar apotheosis. Manifesters of some experience obtained the power to launch positive energy-tinged ectoplasm with celestial conduit and temporarily become half-celestial with planar embrace. Masters of this mantle obtained the power of greater glory, giving good-aligned creatures the upper hand in battle against evil-aligned creatures.

## Goroth

Goroth was a thief of the Moonclaws gang in the early 3 century DR.

Description
He had a flat-eyed face with a cruel demeanor; he kept a fringe beard, and his red hair short-cropped. His gang wore black leathers, with a crossed dagger and moon emblem on the chest.

Abilities
He wielded a bow.

Possessions
He had an archer's bracer, and a metal plate over his gut, under the leathers.

Relationships
He was a member of the Moonclaws. Rival thief Farl knew his name, strongly implying they had met.

History
The first encounter that Farl and Eladar the Dark (the incognito Prince Elminster Aumar of Athalantar) had with the Moonclaws was at a house they were burgling, where they were set upon by a trio of thieves entering through the window. Goroth was the only man among those three thieves. They tried to muscle Farl and Eladar out of their loot, but Eladar hurled Goroth out of a window after a short struggle; rather than try and contest him again, Goroth escaped.

He later participated in the ambush in which they attempted to slay the Velvet Hands guild Eladar and Farl had just founded. He was killed in that ambush; both Eladar and Farl recognized him afterwards.

## Goewin Wood

Goewin Wood was a remote and wild woodland in the southwest of the isle of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
It was a deciduous forest that continued from the undead-haunted western reaches of the largely evergreen Llyrath Forest on the southern coast of Gwynneth. It lay south of Kimball Moor and east of Mere Adammair and the Dwyvach Salt Flats.

Inhabitants
The forest and surrounding wilderness south of Kimball Moor were home to nomadic tribes of shifters. These tribes venerated a being known as the Cat Lord who dwelt within the heart of Goewin Wood.

Notable Locations
 Glade of the Cat Lord, a meeting place for the shifter followers of the Cat Lord to perform bloody rites in his honor.
 Tetauctaa, a village just inside the southern edge of the woods.
 A moonwell lay in the far south of the forest just east of Tetauctaa.

## Gordy

Gordy was a powerful wood woad in the King's Forest of Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, the drow destroyed a keep in the King's Forest that was atop a locus of magic. The site was then taken over by werewolf bandits, who were consequently driven out by Arganon Pine and the Purple Dragons, who created a spell ward to keep the werewolves away. All this activity drew the attention of malevolent druids, who sought to capture the magical site for themselves and use it to control the area. While the War Wizard Zenyff Bravehand created a new ward against the forces of nature, the wood woad Gordy led a final attack on the defenders that ultimately failed.

## Golo Manks

Golo Manks was a human performer and Harper agent who worked in Eveningstar in Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Golo was a heavyset man with a clean shaven face and head, dark skin, and a white outfit.

History
Around the time of the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon during the mid-1480s DR, Golo was part of Rina Belostos's Harper cell. When Oriphaun Huntsilver stole the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll from the shade lord Tyvrus in Wheloon Prison, Golo joined the pursuit of the Nether Scroll and Oriphaun, hoping to keep both out of the hands of Returned Netheril. The group stopped in Eveningstar for a time during the invasion from Sschindylryn, and searched up and down the High Road for clues while posing as a traveling band of entertainers. Golo helped sell the cover by performing alongside Vindavere Dyrwool as they both played the drums for the locals such as Trella Fulnor and Brinbard Meddle.

## Gods of the four directions

Venerated among the wu-jen of T'u Lung, these four deities were each associated with one of the four cardinal directions and one of the five elemental building blocks of matter, but collectively held dominion over the wind. It was believed that proper veneration of them was essential to mastering elemental air.

Culture
In addition to the five elements, T'u Lung culture traditionally associated a particular animal, color, clade of creatures, grain, organ, number, season, smell, and taste to each of the cardinal directions. Memorizing these "correlates" was considered by some wu jen to be a necessary component in mastering magic.

CorrelatesEastSouthCenterNorthWestAnimalSheepFowlOxPigDogColorAzureRedYellowBlackWhiteCreaturesScaly creaturesFeathered creaturesHairless creaturesShelled creaturesHairy creaturesElementWoodFireSoilWaterMetalGrainWheatBeansMilletRiceHempNumber87569OrganSpleenLungsHeartKidneysLiverSeasonSpringSummerFalseWinterAutumnSmellGoatishBurningFragrantRottenRankTasteSourBitterSweetSaltyAcrid

Members
Chu Jung The god of the South. They were traditionally depicted as some form of beast with the face of a human, riding upon the backs of two flaming snakes that had the faces of human women. Their direction was associated with fire.
Ju Shou The god of the West. He was traditionally depicted as a human male that wore a snake on his left ear and rode upon the backs of two lung dragons. His direction was associated with metal.
Kou Mang The god of the East. They were traditionally depicted with the body of a bird and the face of a human. Like Ju Shou, they were also depicted riding upon two lung dragons. Their direction was associated with wood.
Yu Chiang The god of the North. He was traditionally depicted as a human with black wings, resembling those of a hawk, and feathers in place of hair. A small green snake was worn on each of his ears and a larger green snake was coiled around each of his legs. His direction was associated with water.

Appendix
Background
These four deities are most likely based upon the Four Symbols of Chinese mythology. These are four mythical creatures or gods who are each closely associated with a cardinal direction and color, but additionally represent other aspects of life, including one of the annual seasons, an emotion, a virtue, and one of the Chinese "five elements". Furthermore, some descriptions of these deities denote them as riding upon two dragons.

Based upon their respective descriptions and cardinal directions, the inspirations for these Kara-Turan gods can be further narrowed down to the following members of the Four Symbols: Chu Jung to Zhurong, Kou Mang to Goumang, Ju Shou to Rushou, and Yu Chiang to Yuqiang. Additionally, the god of the North is often depicted as black tortoise, which matches up with the list of correlates assigning both "black" and "shelled creatures" to the direction "North".

As part of the philosophy Wuxing the Four Symbols were traditionally accompanied by a fifth deity, Houtu or the Yellow Dragon, representing the center of the universe. Though no such deity is mentioned in this group, Kara-Tur: The Eastern Realms still presents a derivative set-of-five system of correlations, as shown above, with the color "yellow" likewise listed as being associated with the "center".

Within Wuxing, the elements that made up the world are defined as wood, fire, earth, metal, and water. This is copied in the list of correlations above, though it's incongruous with the five elements presented for Kara-Tur in Oriental Adventures, which replaced "earth" with "wind" so as to better align itself with the previously established four elements of Dungeons & Dragons.

## Gotha's Island

Gotha's Island was the name given to a small island within the Moonshae Isles on the edge of the Trackless Sea as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Geography
Gotha's Island was one of the northernmost of the Moonshae Isles (discounting the northern parts of the Korinn Archipelago), located north of the Norheim Isles, Grayrock, and Dragonshome.

The island was largely barren and little more than a pile of rocks that sat not far above the waterline. Toward the center was a low hill containing a cave which opened into a natural cavern that connected to a vast network of sea caves. Parts of the island were pastureland suitable for animals to graze.

History
In the, the dracolich Gotha arrived on the island, and swiftly annihilated its inhabitants and destroyed all of its boats in service to the will of his master, Talos. He was soon joined by the sahuagin King Sythissal of Kressilacc.

Inhabitants
Prior to the arrival of Gotha, the island was inhabited by isolated Northlanders who fished and kept sheep. The largest settlement comprised only about a dozen huts. After Gotha's slaughter, he left most of the structures intact (save for those in which families had attempted to hide, which he had incinerated) and threw the corpses of all the humans and livestock into the sea.

Notable Locations
 Gotha's lair was located in the cave atop the central hill, where the dracolich had excavated to connect it to the sea caves below.

## Gold Lion Wheelwrights

The Gold Lion Wheelwrights was a wheel-making business based in Saelmur in the Land of the Lions in the late 14 century DR.

Though there were no outward signs, the business was directly controlled by the leadership of the Rundeen, a mercantile organization, known as the Yrshelem. The only clue was that it was named for the gold lion, a Cormyrian gold piece, indicating it had once been an independent business that had been taken over and renamed.

## Goodwife Thintagast

Goodwife Thintagast was an inhabitant of Ieirithymbul in the late 14 century DR.

Relationships
She was married to the jeweler Thintagast.

History
In Uktar of the, there was an incident in Thintagast's home where one of his apprentices, Burlmarr, briefly passed out and banged his head on the ground. Goodwife Thintagast assisted in watching over the convalescing gnome until he woke up.
